# Community research in Nocturne recovery

<!-- parallel-recovery-current -->
Current compiled recovery is **3,089/9,065 recognized PS2 functions (34.076%)**, **701 C source units**, and **561,944 recognized body bytes**. Seven Magatama entry functions add 1,020 bytes. Their 376 original worlds and752 strict staged executions pass; the actual701 main passes376 new worlds plus10,336 affected file/menu preservation cases. Two explicit host memory ports route the real guest address space; all game C/H implementations and697 other parent objects are unchanged.

The tests retain real allocator/file-queue publication,3,200 RNG draws,160 sound calls and928 native SYS/ERET pairs. Four Unity roles are authenticated; three native roles remain descriptive. Three ordinary PCs are unvisited, with their source-cold conditions recorded. Queued callback2144A0, full menu process shutdown and physical resource completion remain separate work.

The latest broad regression remains **413 functions /462,684 cases on its actual699-unit build**. Parallel recovery continues in menu process/resource callbacks, model attachment, and battle-result shutdown/field return. See the [workstream index](parallel-recovery-workstreams.json) and [entry admission](magatama-entry-admission.json).

earlier object files remain unchanged; 11,370 retained focused cases and the

unrecovered compendium registration/list routines and two actual sorting

### Full fusion source and research, native validation complete

The [29-routine Unity/community study](fusion-core-HD-community-study.json)
corroborates roles across the complete birth path: normal and special selection,
element/Mitama handling, sacrifice EXP, inheritance, moon/item checks and final
unit construction. These are role counterparts; managed objects, extra output
parameters and bounds exceptions do not establish the PS2 ABI or behavior.
In particular, native inherited-skill lookup can read the adjacent attribute
area when its search reaches index 24. The managed version's bounds behavior
must not replace that original path.

[NMarkro's pinned notes](https://github.com/nmarkro/Nocturne-Randomizer/blob/f74e85c0f632b751eaf278046884961c618c8541/notes/nocturne_random.txt)
independently identify the special-pair table at `0032DB78` and the fusion
selection roles at `001FE690` and `001FE1A0`. The same revision's
[race generator](https://github.com/nmarkro/Nocturne-Randomizer/blob/f74e85c0f632b751eaf278046884961c618c8541/races.py)
uses a reduced, modified race matrix, removes some race categories and changes
Vile/Fury to Avatar. Its result solver has an optional fallback and a different
threshold representation. It is useful context, not a retail expected-output
oracle. All comparison tables come from the original ELF.

The [independent source review](fusion-core-independent-source-review.json)
covers all 1,790 instruction words. The [input and runner review](fusion-core-capture-review.json)
checks 3,764 authored cases, including 384 full birth calls and 31 follow-ups.
The full path needs larger stack backing, additional genuine table rows and an
initialized buffered FILE for its real diagnostic branch. The linked original/C
proofs and publication establish the finite tested domain; these preparations alone do not award recovery credit.

The first original run reached a five-million-step limit for rank selection
with ID 169 and direction 3. The [bounded observation and loop study](fusion-core-rank-direction-divergence.json)
separate that measurement from the source invariant: unsupported directions
leave the step zero, and an excluded current candidate is checked repeatedly.
The recovered source retains this behavior. Returning comparisons admit an
unsupported direction only when the unchanged candidate passes the original
eligibility filters; supported directions still exercise the real scans.
The [SMT transfer candidates](fusion-core-transfer.json) retain seven source
fingerprints with title-specific table, layout and ABI limits. They award no
identity credit to another game.

The [combat calculation publication](combat-calculation-publication.json) adds **34 routines /9,292 recognized bytes /9,392 span bytes**, bringing recovery to **2,509/9,060 functions (27.693%)**, **411,364 recognized integrated bytes** and **855,990 aggregated registered comparisons** (393,306 focused /462,684 broad; 185 focused reports). The canonical main DLL links 495 source units. All [1,220 original-instruction comparisons](combat-calculation-main-C-validation.json) pass in both standalone and main builds over 628,554 original steps: 1,216 returning cases and four genuine enemy-initiative BREAK7 observations. These include 864 defined raw V0 results, 128 raw F0 results and 32 raw F12 inputs.

Current checks pass [13,476 affected cases](combat-calculation-main-focused-regressions.json) across six retained suites and the [413-function /462,684-case broad replay](combat-calculation-main-full-regression.json). All [34 native interfaces](combat-calculation-native-ghidra.json) were saved, freshly reopened and exported with the original processor. All earlier C/header bytes remain unchanged. The source covers hit/effect decisions, HP/MP changes, status recovery, rewards, initiative, escape, consecutive encounters and HP-based model scaling.

[Unity and community research](next-subsystem-after-unit-progression.json) provides 32 qualified HD role leads and two native dependency roles. Randomizer encounter layouts and the independent True Pierce mod supplied useful context; native instructions establish PS2 behavior. The shared zero-return HD body does not establish a unique symbol match. [SMT transfer fingerprints](combat-calculation-transfer.json) record layouts, RNG order and arithmetic distinctions as cross-title search leads. No DDS identity credit is claimed.

The semantic proof checks all 13 declared RAM spans, defined integer/float results, actual completion and traps, Status, condition-profile FCSR, CPU primitives and ordered library/indirect traces. Complete volatile banks, guest private frames, HI1/LO1, arithmetic FCSR exception flags, timing, physical hardware and exhaustive inputs remain outside this proof. Player-initiative coefficient indexes 0..5 are backed; other indexes read private native frame data and remain unresolved. The 127 registered dependencies receive no new identity credit.

The [next battle-action investigation](next-subsystem-after-combat-calculation.json) follows the full SetAllPacket consumer and 20 known unrecovered bodies /9,920 recognized bytes, including three effect constructors reached by callbacks. Their descriptor resources require genuine initialization before nonempty whole-consumer captures. The eight original effect assets identify 19 further unrecovered resource-preparation bodies /3,252 bytes, giving a combined known source target of 39 bodies /13,172 bytes. Further resource-selected callbacks, lifecycle/I/O behavior and the larger SetAction_SKILL workflow remain explicit research work, with no new recovery credit.

Selected payload hashes and archive bounds were checked; the entire archive image was not hashed or copied.

## Special-polygon research and current validation

[The selected family research](drafts/model-context-anchor/special-polygon-pipeline-leads.json)
pins 19 substantive Unity exports and five DDS source/header/symbol files at
revision `bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c`. The community
[polygon manager](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/src/dds1/effect/polyManager.c),
[effect manager](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/src/dds1/effect/effManager.c),

and [DDS symbols](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/config/dds1/symbol_addrs.txt)
provide record and family context. Original Nocturne instructions determine
each native offset, divisor, call and read/store order.

The inspected band records contain age and floating delta; roll records use
age and integer radius; sphere records add delta and two rotations. The native
RollRing init/move pair corresponds to HD methods named SphereRing, while
native SphereRing corresponds to HD RollRing. The names cannot be transferred
without the body checks. Nocturne's sphere callback retains duration one,
where the DDS source has a duration-below-two bypass. Its initializer's
group-zero DIVU precedes a late BREAK and earlier stores remain observable;
the managed zero-group branch instead writes phase zero.

The complete 188-byte Nocturne old-ring move at `001685E0` exactly matches
DDS `0015DDC8`. The DDS 192-byte adjacent-symbol interval includes four
trailing zero padding bytes, checked independently against both ELF images.
The [transfer recorder](../tooling/record_special_polygon_native_transfer.py)
records the complete native span with that extent qualification and zero
recovery credit. Shared billboard/PCP batching and these exact spans are useful
SMT transfer leads; resource offsets, globals, native ABI and completion/colour
behavior still require individual checks.

[All 544 original-certified special-polygon cases](drafts/model-context-anchor/effect-special-polygon-pipeline-findings.json)
now pass strict C comparison across all 17 selected roots, with zero aborts.
Every required processor field, declared RAM byte, actual used-stack byte,

returning inputs include nonempty ring/paired-cell paths, frame aliases,

checks current build inputs and retained proof identities. The current index

## Complete triangle emission and render passes (2026-10-14)

[The emission lead report](model-render-emission-leads.json) independently
checks seven complete roots and six real dependencies against DDS. Pinned
Megami-Decomps and DDS3 Model Studio material/mesh research supplies context;
primary PCSX2 VIF/GIF fields establish the inspected raw packet roles. Every
changed root word is a constrained GP/global-address/call relocation.

The report retains a parser disagreement: Model Studio's bit14 `Sign` property
uses signed Int16 decoding when set, while PCSX2 identifies that bit as USN
and selects unsigned unpacking. The source emits the original raw bytes;
the community property does not become an assumed hardware decoder. MSCAL4
is not evidence of a VU1 upload and is distinct from the recovered VU0 0/9
microprogram. Unity supplies vocabulary rather than these packet algorithms.

[Recovered findings](model-render-emission-findings.md) add seven complete
bodies /2,224 recognized bytes, 3,584 fresh cases/all 508 measured addresses,
1,344 overlaps and 448 connected emission sequences. Thirty-two cases each
make material-mode and parent-flag reloads observable after UV writes. Real
UV/light/colour callees, first/later batch state, ordered quad copies and the
auxiliary final normal prefetch compare. Reopened interfaces, current exports
and 21,760 affected regressions pass. Qualified shared SMT emission patterns
enter the ledger. Upstream clipping builders, DMA/VU consumption, the full
renderer and hardware packet execution remain pending.

## Research scope

The target remains full or near-full reconstruction of the original PS2
executable. Reference work now includes community randomizers, format parsers,
modding notes, annotated patches and public research discussions alongside Unity
HD. Before starting a subsystem, search those sources for existing discoveries
and follow their useful references. Incorporate corroborated information into
the current function/type mappings, asset audits and recovery priorities.

Original `SLUS_209.11` bytes and instructions decide PS2 behavior. Community
names and field interpretations are attributed leads until independently
checked. Keep build-specific addresses, HD additions and modified behavior
distinct. Shared SMT findings still require comparison with the other game's
executable. The no-checkpoint workflow remains in effect.

The source inventory is open-ended. Follow citations and author credits from
randomizers, format tools and patches to original research; search again when a
new subsystem exposes unfamiliar formats, fields or compiler patterns. Record
inaccessible or uninspected sources as follow-up leads rather than accepted
evidence. Useful discoveries should change recovery priorities, annotations,
types or validation inputs, with their provenance retained alongside the result.

Use community findings as independent discovery leads even when Unity offers no
counterpart. In particular, randomizer implementations and battle-script format
research can expose original PS2 tables, script storage and command consumers
that fuzzy function matching alone would miss. Checked leads may change the
next recovery target as well as clarify a function already under investigation.

## Independent model-tool review, 2026-10-12

Added [DDS3 Model Studio](https://github.com/tge-was-taken/DDS3-Model-Studio/tree/92f350749ab24dcfd7c35b8fac105a5c1883d771)
as an independent format reference. Four source files were inspected at revision
`92f350749ab24dcfd7c35b8fac105a5c1883d771`; direct links and hashes are retained in
the rolling [model-tool audit](community-model-studio-audit.json). No upstream
code was executed or copied into recovered source.

Its PIB metadata layout agrees with all 625 inspected original retail PIB
records: BOM `FFFFFFFE`, twenty-byte metadata and effect-group/effect/motion
counts at payload offsets `0C`/`0E`/`10`. Its 64-byte PB record alignment also
agrees with the census. A meaningful interpretation difference remains:
`ModelPackInfo` calls payload `+08` a signed halfword `modelCount`, while the
native getter at `001DEA58` loads a signed full word and registration at
`001DDF54` tests it greater than zero to select the modern path. Every inspected
retail value is `00000001`; that corpus cannot resolve the naming difference.
The native effect/motion getters use unsigned halfwords; the community reader
uses signed ones. Preserve the native widths and decision, including boundary
validation, instead of importing the tool's field semantics wholesale.

The review supplies small unchanged PB candidates for the ongoing in-memory
loader and part-construction work, beginning with `model/event/e659/hikari.PB`
and `model/event/e635/field_a.PB`. Enumerate every record kind before declaring
them texture-free, since the current asset census omits TXP details from each
file's selected record list. Model Studio also mentions broken texture offsets
in `f021_aljira.PB`; its game/build is unspecified, so inspect our original
TXP boundaries before adopting that workaround. Its effect-info reader exposes
raw halfwords, which does not replace our checked typed PIB command consumers.
This review adds context and validation priorities, with no recovery credit.

## Community references for battle presentation recovery

The [presentation-command research audit](battle-presentation-community.json)
checked all eleven then-remaining embedded-BF command descriptors against the pinned
SMT3 library and original ELF. They account for 443 static uses; its pre-recovery inventory contained 41 direct
native bodies / 3,632 bytes at the recovery boundary. Original JAL and
argument-selector words corroborate the library's seven float parameters for
`BTL_CAM_SET` and one integer parameter for `BTL_EVENT`. Per-procedure static
uses now connect these commands to the unchanged original BF corpus for later
execution comparisons. This is research evidence, with no new compiled recovery
credit or runtime coverage.

The pinned [BF format reference](https://github.com/tge-was-taken/Atlus-Script-Tools/blob/1c557a092dd7610829b7215be0f0f5101acf1d72/Documents/Reference/FlowScriptBFFormatReference.txt)
and the randomizer's [Nocturne script assembler](https://github.com/nmarkro/Nocturne-Randomizer/blob/f74e85c0f632b751eaf278046884961c618c8541/nocturne_script_assembler.py)
agree on all 35 opcode numbers. Their general descriptions need native
qualifications: Nocturne has 24-byte label names in 32-byte records, whereas the
reference's Persona variants use longer names. The generic `fileSize` description
also does not account for this BF's trailing 0xF0-byte string section. The
randomizer reads eight bytes for a label offset, combining the native four-byte
offset and trailing word; all 2,894 inspected original label trailing words are
zero. Use section-derived bounds and the native record layout when following
these community leads. The audit records revisions and content hashes without
copying the upstream repositories or original assets.

| [demonsword2's Save State Hacking Guide](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ps2/582958-shin-megami-tensei-nocturne/faqs/75971) | Indexed character HP/MP, stats, skills and inventory section; direct retrieval restricted | Follow-up vocabulary/layout lead for player work and inventory; reported savestate offsets are not treated as PS2 RAM addresses |

The added savestate guide remains a follow-up lead. Its build and offset basis
need checking against the savestate format and native consumers before importing
addresses, field names or widths. The indexed excerpt also notes equipment
bonuses in reported stats, which may help distinguish saved values from derived
battle values. No source recovery credit is added from this guide.

The randomizer credits TGEnigma, Krisan Thyme and Zombero. Its implementation
and notes are research inputs, not changes to apply to the original game.
Its generated patch labels identify patch sites and injected routines, not a
complete original symbol table. The old Zombero WordPress homepage currently
serves unrelated content; use the original discussion for these claims.

## Additional original-research lead: native debug paths

[AceKombat's public USA debug notes](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/boards/582958-shin-megami-tensei-nocturne/68210184)
identify control address `0054E824`. The [reproducible static audit](community-debug-audit.json)
checks eleven original word loads in ten currently exported functions, all at
`GP-594C` with `GP=00554170`. This independently corroborates the existing
debug-enable binding and the gate in `nbMainProcess_SetDebugFlag`; other readers
provide follow-up leads for native debug and battle paths.

The post's options code points to `0012167C`, where the retail instruction is
`addiu a0,zero,6` inside `001215F0`, followed by a call to `00102848`.
Treat this as an instruction-immediate lead. The reported selector16/17
event-viewer/test meanings need dispatch and caller investigation. No patch or
runtime state change was made, and these observations add no recovery count.
The audit retains source attribution and original words; indexed retrieval does
not establish a complete source-page capture or reproduce the author's runtime
observations.

Save-state guides and other public notes remain discovery leads until their
actual relevant material is inspected; merely finding a search result does not
make its layouts or claims accepted evidence.

## Verified new lead: embedded battle AI

The community discussion points to an AI script at ELF file offset `0x3F1800`
with extent `0x35BF1`. Our executable contains an `FLW0` BF resource there,
mapped to RAM address **`0x004F0800`** by its actual load segment. This resource
was outside the earlier audit of **191 DDS3-indexed BF files**.

The current [static audit](community-research-audit.json), refreshed on
2026-10-12 with `tooling/audit_community_research.py`, checks the original bytes
directly. The refreshed command-library checks retain zero active parameter
disagreements, and all embedded-script command targets now have current function
exports. Export availability does not establish complete source recovery or
execution coverage. The resource census remains:

- 141 procedure records, including `ai_0`, `ai_test`, `ai_1` and named helpers.
- 2,753 branch-label records and 28,208 code words, decoding as 27,795
  instructions after skipping the extra words of immediate instructions.
- 7,178 static `COMM` occurrences using 63 command IDs. Procedure/branch
  operands are in range; linear decoding encounters no unknown opcodes.
- 29 command IDs absent from the previous archive script usage inventory.
  Together, the two inventories contain 394 used command IDs. These counts are
  static occurrences, not execution frequencies or proof of reachable paths.
- Original descriptors identified **`00288C90`**, command376, community name
  `BTL_AI_ROT_CLR`, without an existing function export. Its ownership and
  twelve-byte body are now checked, recognized and recovered in the
  [battle AI batch](battle-ai-actions-findings.md).

The BF header's declared size is **`0x35B01`**; its string section starts there
and adds `0xF0` bytes. Its section-derived extent is therefore **`0x35BF1`**,
agreeing with the community location/extent. A parser that reads only the
declared size would omit that string section. Preserve the native header and
check section ranges against the backed resource when adding this corpus to
construction and execution coverage.

This is the same BF format family as field/event scripts, with battle commands
in the native dispatch table. The basic weighted AI records discussed below
are a separate data structure. Startup is recovered in the
[startup batch](battle-ai-startup-findings.md); the native record-to-procedure
bridge is investigated below. Original decision selection and actual battle
execution of complete original BF decision paths still needs verification.

## Command library checked against PS2 descriptors

The inspected [SMT3 command library](https://github.com/tge-was-taken/Atlus-Script-Tools/blob/1c557a092dd7610829b7215be0f0f5101acf1d72/Source/AtlusScriptLibrary/Libraries/SMT3/Modules/Common/Functions.json)
contains 544 entries. Reading our original descriptor table at `0x0052E350`
finds 438 nonzero targets, all with parameter counts agreeing with the library.
All 426 previously inspected target/count pairs also agree with the ELF.

The remaining 106 slots have null PS2 targets. Eight parameter disagreements
are in those null slots and have `HD_*` names. Do not create PS2 functions or
infer missing original behavior from those HD entries. Matching parameter
counts corroborate the mapping; they do not independently prove each name,
return type or command semantics.

These command-library leads have native target/count checks. Action and
rotation roles below are subsequently recovered using Unity and original
instructions; automatic targeting is now covered by the
[selection recovery](battle-ai-selection-findings.md). See the current
registered reports for compiled membership:

| Command ID | Original entry | Community name | Static use in embedded AI |
|---|---|---|---:|
| 51 / `0x33` | `00287E08` | `AI_ACT_SKILL` | 1,234 |
| 52 / `0x34` | `00287EE0` | `AI_TAR_AI` | 1,155 |
| 10 / `0x0A` | `0010B908` | `RND` | 450 |
| 48 / `0x30` | `00287D78` | `AI_ACT_ATTACK` | 205 |
| 376 / `0x178` | `00288C90` | `BTL_AI_ROT_CLR` | 6 |

For the existing field-command frontier, command96 at `00158218` is named
`PAD_DISABLE` in the toolchain and described as removing field control by the
randomizer. Command97 at `00158258` is `PAD_ENABLE`; both have zero native
arguments. Use these independent leads alongside their Unity bodies and native
callees. The library also corroborates the already-reviewed command18
`CAM_SEL`; the obsolete `CALL_EVENT` lead remains rejected.

## Data and patch leads to investigate

[Randomizer notes](https://github.com/nmarkro/Nocturne-Randomizer/blob/f74e85c0f632b751eaf278046884961c618c8541/notes/nocturne_random.txt)
explicitly target `SLUS_209.11`. These are **ELF file offsets**, not executable
function addresses. For the relevant backed segments in our ELF, RAM addresses
are file offsets plus `0xFF000`; derive this from the segment rather than
assuming it applies to another build.

| Community record lead | File offset | RAM address | Community stride / count |
|---|---|---|---|
| Demon records | `0024A7F0` | `003497F0` | `0x3C` / 383 |
| Demon skill-growth records | `00234CF4` | `00333CF4` | `0x66` / demon index |
| Basic enemy AI records, implemented base | `002999E0` | `003989E0` | `0xA4` / demon index |
| Encounter records | `002AFFE0` | `003AEFE0` | `0x26` / 1,270 |

The notes give AI start `0x2999E4`, but `nocturne.py` uses `0x2999E0` and retains
the former address as a commented alternative. The
[original-reader audit](community-engine-audit.json) now resolves this:
`00253C40` reads `LHU(0x003989E0 + demon_id * 0xA4)` and passes that procedure
index into native BF startup `00288D80`. `002867A8` reads decision words at
record offsets `+4` and `+8`; `00286B68` reads a targeting selector at `+2`.
Thus the notes' `+4` location is the beginning of decision data, rather than
the full record. This conclusion checks the multiply/load instruction words,
not just Ghidra's generated data labels.

The randomizer's demon IDs run from 1 through 383; its displayed demon-record
base is the first such record, while AI addressing uses `base + id * stride`.
The audit inspects those 383 AI rows plus native row0. All 384 procedure
halfwords index one of the 141 embedded BF procedures; the targeting selector
is zero throughout this inspected original domain. This does not establish
the allocation's full extent. The subsequent
[basic AI recovery](battle-ai-execution-findings.md) checks the three condition
pairs and three weighted action tables against original readers and unchanged
records; the final four-byte word remains uninterpreted.

## Shared-engine and compiler research

The pinned [DDS/Persona 4 transfer report](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/docs/p4-transfer.md)
is now an additional source lead. It reports 491 pairs associated with matched
P4 source, but only 41 byte-identical recompilations with the DDS toolchain;
those include trivial stubs and inline assembly. This distinguishes structural
pairing from usable reconstructed C. Follow the listed scheduler, script,
message and random/VU implementations with their original header/compiler
context, then verify Nocturne's bytes, relocations, callers and layouts. The
upstream compilation/link results have not been reproduced here and add no
Nocturne recovery credit. Revision and content hash are retained in
[the engine audit](community-engine-audit.json).

The [DDS community project's source-unit notes](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/docs/tu-names.md)
describe using `__FILE__` paths from the December26 2002 JP Nocturne debug build
`SLPM_652.42` to project source-file names through related builds. The reported
172 paths and 1,478 code references are upstream evidence: we have not inspected
that executable here. Keep source-unit labels provisional, especially where
they are interpolated between anchors. Their tool masks many low16 immediates,
which can also conceal changes to non-address constants; that comparison alone
does not meet our standard for accepting complete shared function behavior.

Our [community engine audit](community-engine-audit.json) instead checks each
changed word in four complete spans against the existing original Nocturne and
DDS1 executables. DDS1's SHA-1 agrees with the project's supported USA build.

| Nocturne RAM entry | DDS1 RAM entry | Community alias | Checked span / differences |
|---|---|---|---|
| `00101978` | `00101938` | `kwlnTaskFindByPriority` | 140 bytes; three queue-head GP relocations |
| `00101898` | `00101858` | `kwlnTaskGetTaskByName` | 220 bytes; the same three queue relocations |
| `00101AB0` | `00101A70` | `kwlnTaskGetUserValue` | 8 identical bytes, constrained by surrounding scheduler context |
| `00101AF8` | `00101A80` | `func_00101A80` | 128 bytes; one unlink-callee JAL relocation |

The source/types corroborate queue order, priority at `+20`, scheduler next at
`+3C`, process user data at `+38`, and parent/child/sibling links at
`+44/+48/+4C`. Preserve the already reviewed HD names in our mappings; these
community aliases supply independent context, not original Atlus spelling.
No upstream implementation is executed or copied into recovered source.

The [compiler notes](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/docs/toolchain.md)
identify DDS game/engine code with ee-gcc2.96, `-O2`, Sony ee-as and `-G8`, using
compiler comparisons and instruction patterns. That gives us a concrete lead
for a future Nocturne matching build; it does not turn the current host C build
or instruction comparisons into a matching PS2 executable. The project's
[battle-table notes](https://github.com/Megami-Decomps/dds-decomp/blob/bec33411543cdc487bad9debdf758f0090a2242c/docs/battle-tables.md)
also provide related-engine context for DDS AI/formula BFs and weighted tables.
Recheck Nocturne's actual formats before carrying over those schemas.

The audit records the inspected revision, URLs and SHA-256 of twelve upstream
inputs, original executable hashes, individual relocation words and AI header
rows. It retains findings rather than a repository clone or duplicate assets.

The script-ID and flag notes can connect executable commands to recognizable
game scenes and provide original-script execution cases. The patch sources
give focused code locations for skill inheritance, recruitment race checks,
healing restrictions and Pierce handling. Before incorporating any of them,
inspect the original instructions and distinguish the mod's replacement
behavior from what the original function does.

The initial research pass added scope and static leads without promoting
function counts. The subsequent [battle AI recovery](battle-ai-actions-findings.md)
adds eleven compiled bodies and reviewed Unity roles using original-instruction
evidence. The later [startup/message-variable batch](battle-ai-startup-findings.md)

variable-layout variant. The later [battle-work batch](battle-work-access-findings.md) adds six bodies,
five Unity roles and a strict complete DDS priority-search span; attributed
community research resolves the AI header boundary. Original gameplay execution
remains unproved. Source and reports
are updated in place; no checkpoint, project clone or asset backup is created.

## Packed render stream loops and normalization

The [stream audit](model-render-stream-leads.json) rechecks seven complete
original Nocturne/DDS spans totaling 524 bytes; all are byte-identical.
Pinned [DDS3 Model Studio mesh readers](https://github.com/tge-was-taken/DDS3-Model-Studio/tree/92f350749ab24dcfd7c35b8fac105a5c1883d771/Source/DDS3ModelLibrary/Models/MeshTypes)
corroborate Vector3 ordinary/blend position and normal arrays, versus Vector4
weighted positions with Vector3 normals. Native loads determine the raw Z
sign-extension W word, including negative zero, and the 96-byte output stride.
Original caller checks distinguish copy, delta blend and weighted paths.

The odd first-weighted-point helper branches on A0 after adding 96 and stores
at advanced A3 minus 96. Initial A0=-96*k gives a bounded constructed original
path; A1 does not determine its termination. This finding permits testing the
unchanged body and does not establish that retail callers supply these values.
The native normalizer uses incoming VF1.x in x²+y²+VF1.x*z², then VRSQRT with
VF0.w numerator and VF4.x denominator. Standalone tests retain that dependence.
Pinned PCSX2 instruction sources corroborate selectors and value semantics;
pipeline and hardware behavior remain separate.

The current Unity search finds shared return-only model exports and managed
motion blends, which are not exact identities for these native streams. That
negative search remains bounded to inspected names and corpus records. The
community layouts and original instructions now underpin [seven recovered
bodies](model-render-streams-findings.md), 3,584 passing comparisons, 2,080
overlaps, 20,160 independent oracles and connected copy/blend/normalize cases.
At that milestone, continue the remaining four clip/light helpers and complete renderer/placement
paths. No full executable, retail odd-path or physical hardware claim is made.

prefetches the next record before current light/flag stores, even on its last
iteration. These are instruction-derived constraints on reconstruction.

1003-body diagnostic has been rerun with these repaired inputs; its runtime
result remains separate from pure constructor validation and C equivalence.
This run returned 35 metadata rows over 2,319,807 instruction steps without
memory warnings, including both formerly failing actor consumers. It stopped
before supplying WaitSema(10) in `0024FCC0` through `00263250` /`00198200` /
`00197DD0`. The nested caller has the cold reference count and empty resource
registry but lacks the genuine model-registration prefix present in the six
direct model tests. The original miss path requests real loading through `001DC188` /`001DC8B8`;
it is legitimate, and adding clone replies alone would omit file/worker behavior.
A genuine registration prefix defines a complementary loaded-resource domain,
which must preserve separately authored miss cases and their unresolved IO
obligations. No C case ran.

The [nested-source study](battle-action-skill/nested-model-protocol-study.json),
[independent review](battle-action-skill/nested-model-independent-review.json),
and [all-recipe census](battle-action-skill/model-recipe-coverage-study.json)
separate these domains. `0024FCC0` now uses real model registration in profiles
0-15 and retains lazy loading in profiles 16-31. `0024FE40` currently skips
its clone branch because actor bit 2 is clear; `00255650` remains conditional.
Both full 2,624-image checks found no RAM, stack, bank, or argument changes;
only sixteen actual registration prefixes and their pending-domain markers
were added. Historical source and complete input hashes remain reproducible
by explicit inverses, without retaining fixture copies.

The [whole loaded-root protocol audit](battle-action-skill/nested-loaded-schedule-study.json)
accounts for 68 registration requests, two lookup requests, and 34 palette-copy
requests in profile zero. The remaining 32 packet bodies contain no syscall;
the only indirect site is the already checked pool allocator. The action queues
five packet kinds without executing their callbacks, so no additional upload
schedule is inferred from those queued records. Seven model lists and eight
font lists now total 808 explicit diagnostic requests. Exact whole-list runtime
consumption remains required; missing-resource/file/worker profiles remain
unadmitted and the full 82-by-32 scope is preserved.

The next actual original-instruction run completed all 82 selected profile-zero
roots over 3,804,498 steps, with zero memory warnings and no native BREAKs.
All seven model lists /728 requests and eight font lists /80 requests passed
complete-consumption checks before their rows were emitted. In particular,
`0024FCC0` returned after 453,478 steps with the genuine registration/clone
path. The [current run review](battle-action-skill/diagnostic-run-review.json)
is a diagnostic completion record, not an original/C equivalence certificate:
these rows lack the complete final-memory and trace fields required for that
proof, no selected C game case has run, and all 2,624 full-suite obligations
remain. Published recovery stays at 2,593/9,060 functions. The next concrete
phase is the reviewed compact full-state observation/C comparison transport.

The [comparison transport plan](battle-action-skill/comparison-transport-plan.json)
keeps full memory-span and stack coverage while proposing compact original
digests and raw bank/trace records for actual C comparison. Diagnostic rows
omit fields needed for this proof, including vector clip/ACC-overflow state,
and cannot be promoted into references. Allowed-write containment and exact
executed-store equality remain distinct claims. The plan is preparatory; no
full82 C comparison has run.

The compact format now has a [pure Java/Python microtest](battle-action-skill/observation-format-microtest.json)
and an [independent format review](battle-action-skill/observation-format-independent-review.json).
The review covered all seven memory spans /9,322,496 bytes, all 2,276 pages,
every byte of the 8 KB dirty stack, and all 304 architectural bank lanes.
A typed-memory-view reader could previously hash more bytes than its declared
extent; it now accepts only contiguous one-dimensional byte buffers of the
exact requested extent. Cross-language framing and raw signed-bit transport
agree. These tests execute no game code and do not yet connect original or C
state readers; full comparison admission remains pending.

A [compact AFTER helper](battle-action-skill/observation-capture-helper-source-review.json)
and [live C adapter](battle-action-skill/actual-adapter-api-test.json) now pass
plain-Java/Python mock checks. The [assertion contract](battle-action-skill/observation-contract.json)
fixes equality roles before any pilot comparison: all memory and stack remain
mandatory, saved integer registers retain the existing low64 contract, F20-F31
are preserved, and raw volatile/upper lanes remain separately observable.
The adapter audit found that strict C syscalls bypass the legacy combined trace.
The isolated kernel transport now appends actual kind-zero records at call time,
preserving interleaving with DI/EI/SYNC rather than reconstructing order later.
The exact source inverse, four warning-as-error compilations, current isolated
rebuild and [independent linked-module audit](battle-action-skill-probe-linked-independent-review.json)
pass. The [independent adapter review](battle-action-skill/actual-adapter-independent-review.json)

[live BEFORE seed check](battle-action-skill/actual-adapter-live-seed-review.json)
loads that DLL and verifies all seven spans, 9,322,496 bytes, 2,276 pages,
304 bank elements, the full 8,192-byte stack and 28 alias boundary reads.
It executes no guest entry, consumes no kernel input and reads no AFTER reference.
The [independent collector review](battle-action-skill/observation-capture-helper-independent-review.json)
passes thirteen synthetic observations and 58 rejection tests, including every
page and bank-lane mutation. The [compact driver review](battle-action-skill/compact-driver-independent-review.json)
also verifies the complete named register reader and the void-prefix fix:
JSON-null result storage keeps the actual incidental V0, and prefix V0/F0
snapshots occur before the next call's arguments are installed. Its exact
inverse reconstructs the prior driver; primitive computation remains unchanged.

The [fresh original compact pilot](battle-action-skill/compact-original-run-review.json)
now completes all 82 cases with no uninitialized reads or script errors:
378 real prefixes, 808 exact finite kernel records and 3,804,498 steps.
Every case records all seven spans and 2,276 page hashes, 304 raw bank elements,
the complete 8,192-byte stack, actual prefix results and ordered traces.
The rolling observation file is about 16 MB; no full AFTER RAM copy or
checkpoint was created. This establishes complete original-side observations,
not C equivalence. The first actual C pass completed 77 comparisons; five
isolated processes failed before their roots because actual prefix `0016FE88`
was absent from the SKILL dispatcher. The existing canonical
`battle_action_activation.c` contains its complete 41-instruction native
implementation and real 48-byte guest frame. An
[independent route review](battle-action-skill/activation-route-independent-review.json)
verifies the one-header/one-case repair, its exact inverse, the original body,
the real canonical object owner and all 14 unique pilot prefix targets.
The router now contains 297 proved targets, including 106 canonical reuses;
the original body scope and game implementations are unchanged.

The rebuilt isolated module passes the
[complete 82-case pilot](battle-action-skill/compact-C-validation.json):
82 actual C observations, 378 actual prefixes and 808 finite kernel records,
with zero failures under the unchanged pre-execution assertion policy.
Each side independently starts from the same BEFORE inputs. All seven RAM
spans and their 186,632 total page hashes, every full stack and its original
used interval, declared return values, preserved register lanes, VU state,
Status/FCSR, ordered kernel/DI/EI/SYNC/VM traces and primitive counts agree.
All 304 raw bank elements per case remain captured; volatile/upper GPR lanes,
non-return FPRs and final HILO remain diagnostics under the frozen policy.
The canonical DLL is unchanged. The full 2,624-case requirement, native ABI
application/integration and published 2,593-function count remain unchanged.

The [remaining admission study](battle-action-skill/remaining-admission-study.json)
checks 96 pure recipe reconstructions and 53 original instruction witnesses.
The retained lazy-load profiles contain an intentional prior kind1/phase1 job
that cannot complete without worker progress. The actual `.PB` path uses
`1DE220 -> 2968F8`, with callback `1DE1A0 -> 1DDF00 -> 1DC230`; it must not be
confused with the generic texture callback path. Real worker scheduling and
byte arrivals remain required. The original 32 profiles also miss the root
kind11 `400/20/1000` guard matrix. Thirteen named supplements now preserve those
2,624 required cases while adding eight flag combinations, a timeline-zero
control, and four other-actor eligibility controls. The
[actual original-instruction review](battle-action-skill/actor-branch-original-observation-review.json)
records all thirteen: 65 genuine prefixes and 884 consumed external records,
four completed roots and nine strict stops before an unauthored reply. With
skill 1 and the reviewed profile-0 world, classifier `265948` returns 11 only
when Main `+60 & 400`, target unit `+0 & 20` and target actor `+78 & 1000` are
all set; the seven other combinations return 0. Actual guard memory was read
before the native loads. Clearing target-4's timeline halfword at Action `+AC`
prevents the classifier and kind11 handler from executing. The eligible other
actor reaches the original packet/model calls; excluded and inactive controls
complete without those calls. Actor mask `2` is a literal mask, not a proven
universal death-state name. These are Ghidra original-instruction observations
with authored external replies, not live PCSX2 observations or C equivalence.

Seven negative flag cases next request `GetThreadId` in the C-library malloc
lock. The [heap bootstrap study](battle-action-skill/libc-heap-bootstrap-study.json)
shows valid original bin sentinels but a zero lock semaphore and an initial
break at `5A0330`, outside the seven declared RAM spans. Native `2E40D0` creates
the two heap locks; it leaves semaphore attributes/options as stack inputs.
Its complete 18-instruction, 80-byte-frame C reconstruction is
[strictly object-compiled](battle-action-skill-heap-bootstrap-frames-source-review.json)
and linked in a separate heap probe that reuses the existing objects in place.
The [original heap observations](battle-action-skill/heap-original-observation-review.json)
now cover all thirteen supplements with a genuine initializer prefix, an eighth
backed RAM span, and finite authored system replies: 78 native prefixes and
945 external records, four completed roots and nine strict stops. The seven
negative flag cases allocate the first 64-byte Balloc cache at `5A0340`, advance
the break to `5A1000`, and create the expected chunk/top headers themselves.
No allocated object, cache pointer or heap header is supplied as an input.
They next stop before another unauthored `GetThreadId` reply. The
[C initializer comparison](battle-action-skill/heap-bootstrap-C-validation.json)
passes all thirteen cases: all 304 raw state elements, eight whole RAM spans,
the full 8 KiB stack and complete kernel/V1 traces match each original prefix.
This comparison executes only the initializer and its two CreateSema leaves;
it does not establish C equivalence for the later allocation or actor roots.
The subsequent [bounded heap-world run](battle-action-skill/heap-world-run-review.json)
preserves those exact BEFORE inputs and independently resolves only the five
declared external services: thread ID 31, heap ceiling `600000`, two actual
CreateSema packets, and counted Wait/Signal operations. The game still owns
every allocation, cache, owner/depth field and RAM write. Unknown services,
blocked waits and the 4,096-request limit stop before a reply. All thirteen
original cases are now observed with 987 accepted external records: eleven
normal returns and the two retained `WaitSema(10)` stops. Each formerly blocked
negative guard case returns after 81 records with its native heap lock released.

The [independent C world comparison](battle-action-skill/heap-world-C-validation.json)
runs the eleven completed roots using a separately implemented C external
world, not a replay of the original run's generated responses. Four controls
pass in the initial run. All seven negative cases initially returned and matched
every non-stack RAM span, but failed the unchanged complete-stack assertion at
487 bytes each. Native printf called semantic `ps2LibcCvt` at `2D6AD0`; that
helper and its `2D8378` dtoa dependency bypassed their original guest frames.
That gap is now [resolved](battle-action-skill/heap-world-stack-differences.json)
with complete native callee implementations and exact typed aliases in the
isolated printf compilation. The current eleven-case comparison passes all
eleven: all eight RAM spans, all 8 KiB stack bytes, required register/value
roles, six prefix publications and all kernel/primitive/indirect traces agree.
The seven formatter paths also match every captured raw bank element. Four
controls retain diagnostic secondary-HILO differences under the existing policy.
The [complete native-frame study](battle-action-skill/libc-native-frame-gap-study.json)
closes both roots over 43 whole bodies and 3,989 instructions. Fourteen existing
native implementations cover 708 instructions; the reconstructed 29 cover 3,281,
including 21 functions with frames. Their [whole source](../src/battle_action_libc_frames.c)
passes both strict compile profiles and an [independent instruction review](battle-action-libc-frames-independent-review.json).
Sixteen of those 29 entries execute in the eleven actor cases; thirteen have
source/compile evidence only in this stage. The study records actual helper interfaces,
the six-target dtoa switch and primary/secondary integer arithmetic requirements.
The 534-source heap probe includes the explicit world provider and native
libc unit. The initial closure build reused 528 original objects and locally
compiled six; the current diagnostic-capacity build below reuses 527 and
compiles seven. Its default strict mode again passes the thirteen initializer
comparisons.
The two positive model paths stopped at `WaitSema(10)` because the first
external world did not include that semaphore. The
[lookup-mutex study](battle-action-skill/model-lookup-mutex-study.json)
corrects the interpretation: native initializer `1DC0F8` creates the model
registry lock with initial count 1 and maximum 127. In these inputs, the
genuine registration prefix has already loaded model `1:10D`; the subsequent
lookup takes and releases the lock without issuing a file request. Its
36-request tail is the lookup pair followed by seventeen palette-copy pairs.
This differs from the genuine missing-resource profiles, which still need
the native file worker.

The separate [model-world capture](battle-action-skill/model-world-run-review.json)
keeps the exact same thirteen BEFORE rows and game prefixes. Independent

All thirteen original paths return normally, consuming 1,059 external records
and 78 genuine prefixes. Both positive paths consume 106 records and restore

family. Sixteen of the 29 native libc entries are reached; thirteen still

The [worker supplement](battle-action-skill-worker-availability-independent-review.json)
independently checks 48 additional whole bodies and 2,856 original instructions.
The proposed union is 1,051 bodies; this is static availability, not runtime

Six additional syscall boundaries include `AddDmacHandler` with three arguments.
A separate [20-site worker transport](battle-action-skill/worker-kernel-v2-source-review.json)
now represents all three arguments in an explicit 72-byte record and validates

passes seven checks over two contexts: 2,128 raw state elements, actual original
Wait leaves with two explicitly authored external replies, branch-delay/annul

The [SIF dispatcher supplement](battle-action-skill-sif-dispatch-availability.json)
adds two pending whole bodies, `2DE330` and `2DC290`, with 78 instructions.
The actual game dispatcher copies the device packet onto its own 144-byte
frame and invokes the registered handler with the real stack pointer and
registered parameter. Calling an imported handler directly would omit this
behavior. Its distinct `-120` syscall at `2DC294` is now explicitly represented
as the twentieth transport contract, with zero arguments. The prospective
worker/dispatcher union has 1,053 bodies, or 1,054 with the heap initializer.
These are availability counts, not executed worker paths or recovery credit.
The earlier job must be created and completed through genuine manager,
worker and `.PB` callbacks. Neither these actor supplements nor a loaded model
replace the retained lazy-load and earlier-inflight-job cases.
The [minimal worker-bootstrap study](battle-action-skill/worker-bootstrap-study.json)
checks 27 whole bodies and 84 source pins. Manager initializer `297890`, real
request `2968F8` and foreground pump `297870` need six additional bodies beyond
the original 1,003 (or 1,010 with the separately added heap initializer); all six are already in the worker supplement. The manager
creates its process and request ring; the first real enqueue creates the OS
worker through `2BF8F0`. The existing three process names/workspaces need a
coherent ready-list input before insertion. A genuine earlier HANSYA.PB request
is proposed without dropping the in-flight-job axis. Full thread scheduling,
RPC/device completion and callback ownership remain unexecuted here.

The separate [original worker-bootstrap capture](battle-action-skill/worker-bootstrap-observation-review.json)
now executes manager initialization and the first genuine earlier request in
two BEFORE-only cases, with coherent original ready-process links. Both return:
4,506 original instructions, eleven genuine prefixes and fourteen accepted OS
requests. The manager creates a waiting `fileMan` process and queues the actual
`/battle/d3p/HANSYA.PB` request. Its native file job has completion callback
`296D90` and points back to the genuine manager node. The native device setup
allocates a 16 KiB stack and calls CreateThread for `2BE8C8`, then StartThread
with the device record `529B00`. The explicit external world records a deferred
start; it does not execute the worker or complete a file. Nine whole RAM spans,
all 304 raw bank elements and the full 8 KiB foreground stack are retained.
The manager filename is observed; the distinct normalized file-job name has
not yet been read at a real open boundary.

The [primary PS2SDK kernel header](https://github.com/ps2dev/ps2sdk/blob/57d60f4bbde84697652cf316276a3564db549d11/ee/kernel/include/kernel.h#L180)
at revision `57d60f4bbde84697652cf316276a3564db549d11` supplies independent
community context for the semaphore and thread packet layouts. The small
[layout reference](battle-action-skill/worker-kernel-layout-reference.json)
records the inspected bytes and hash. Original `2A8080` writes only semaphore
offsets `+4` (maximum), `+8` (initial count), and `+14` (option). Native earlier
calls overwrite the packet's other words on the real stack, so their nonzero
values must be preserved rather than reset. The original manager packet's
unwritten count and attr words are `224` and `3E003FD0`; those bytes are not
the independently chosen OS model's initial semaphore count. Modern SDK names
explain the layout, while original instructions remain the behavior authority.

Whole native C variants now cover the six bootstrap bodies (370 instructions)
and fourteen path, queue and thread bodies (638 instructions). Both families
pass strict object compilation and independent instruction/ABI/frame review;
the fourteen are already registered functions and receive no new identity
credit. The [C integration study](battle-action-skill/worker-bootstrap-C-world-plan.json)
identifies why native packet producers and explicit 72-byte OS records are
needed: the previous semantic file-request exit constructed a host packet and
could not establish genuine guest-stack behavior. Isolated integration and
original/C comparison are separate from this source evidence. Full worker
execution, SIF/device arrivals, the real foreground pump, genuine completion
callbacks, cold profiles 16–31 and the full 2,624-case family remain required.

The [isolated C comparison](battle-action-skill/worker-bootstrap-C-validation.json)
now passes both bootstrap cases against that original capture. The 540-unit
module reuses 531 actual objects and compiles nine local units; the old observer
stays unchanged. Object code and relocations preserve the retained kernel
after eight symbol aliases and the request family after its one typed native
file-read alias. Seven explicit call routes reach complete native bodies.
All 19,472,384 observed RAM bytes, both full 8 KiB stacks, all fourteen OS
records, exact syscall PCs, full V1 outputs, primitive ordering, real prefix
results and native queue/process/thread lineage agree. Both cases also happen
to match all 304 raw bank elements, beyond the frozen required ABI roles.
Fourteen of the twenty new native variants are entered across these cases;
the six unentered variants retain source and compile evidence only. This is
still startup validation under chosen external OS inputs, not real worker or
device execution. The native module records the worker start as deferred.
The [retained-path regression](battle-action-skill/worker-bootstrap-retained-regressions.json)
also passes all 37 cases on this module: thirteen strict initializer cases,

and their original inputs stay unchanged; only the selected module path/hash
differs. These checks compare 359,481,344 RAM bytes, 303,104 stack bytes and

byte-identical. No checkpoints, backup trees or copied validation vectors
were created.

The [twenty-body DDS1 scan](battle-action-skill/worker-bootstrap-DDS-transfer-leads.json)
adds five unique exact matches and ten unique calls-only candidates, with the

The [first-worker continuation study](battle-action-skill/first-worker-continuation-study.json)
checks the next boundary against the observed thread and current cold inputs.
A minimal bounded original scope adds `2BE8C8`, `2DFD70`, `2DF9D0` and `2DF980`:
476 instructions, for 1,014 available bodies. The real worker must consume its
posted semaphore-24 token and select command 1 through the original table.
Its command byte has already been cleared in a branch delay slot by the
indirect jump, so the guard must check the actual computed table address and
loaded target rather than reread the old command byte. The cold file-RPC
mutex creation is the first unsupported OS request, before DMA/MMIO access.
The bounded path has now run as described below. A caught callback failure
is diagnostic-only: the calibrated context helper
supports completed pre-Wait/STOP boundaries, not resumption inside that failure.

The [first-worker original capture](battle-action-skill/first-worker-run-review.json)
executes 69 worker instructions after the genuine bootstrap request from the
unchanged second input row. The new context uses the same run's accepted
CreateThread/StartThread receipts and live 48-byte native packet: entry
`2BE8C8`, argument `529B00`, GP `554170`, and a 16 KiB stack at `C40000`.
Other initial register values are explicit OS test inputs, not a retail BIOS
claim. The context entry changes no RAM. The worker really consumes semaphore
24, clears command 1, writes executing state 2 and calls native `2DFD70` with
`cdrom0:\BATTLE\D3P\HANSYA.PB;1` and raw arguments `(filename,1,0)`.
It stops at `2DBEA4`, selector 64, before receiving the cold SDK mutex reply.
The actual 24-byte packet at `C43E30` has maximum/initial counts 1 and option 0.
The rejected request leaves all banks, nine RAM spans, accepted records and
external model state unchanged. Its emulator state is `EXECUTE`; this caught
callback image is diagnostic and cannot be resumed as a completed instruction.

Four complete native variants cover all 476 original instruction members,
including later branches not exercised here. Their
[source review](battle-action-skill-first-worker-frames-independent-review.json)
checks real frames, branch delays, the nine-word command table and four raw
indirect callback sites. The latter remain unadmitted. The isolated 543-unit
module reuses 538 objects, compiles five local units and preserves all 4,442

preserve the retained router and OS transport after their sole symbol aliases;
the shared register bank and observer each retain one owner.

The [original/C comparison](battle-action-skill/first-worker-C-validation.json)
passes this same boundary: all 9,736,192 RAM bytes, all 304 stopped register-bank
elements, the full 16 KiB worker and 8 KiB foreground stacks, twelve accepted
OS records, full V1 values, syscall PCs, primitive ordering/counts and indirect
events match. Accepted C events retain foreground context 0 and the actual
worker ID 41, with Status checked against the original ordered trace. Passive
telemetry records the real rejected thirteenth request before delegation; it
supplies no response or model update. Original and C independently evaluate
the external OS model rather than replaying generated original replies.
This is original-instruction/recovered-C execution under a chosen OS world,
not live PCSX2 scheduling or hardware validation. File I/O, SIF/device arrivals,
completion callbacks and the foreground pump remain unexecuted. All four
native variants were already registered; published recovery stays 2,593.
The [four retained comparisons](battle-action-skill/first-worker-retained-regressions.json)
also pass on this 543-unit module: both bootstrap paths, one strict heap
initializer and one positive loaded-model case. They compare 38,903,808 RAM
bytes, 32,768 stack bytes and 122 OS records. The earlier full 37-case result
belongs to the unchanged 540-unit module; it was not repeated on 543.

The [SDK/SIF startup study](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-startup-study.json)
corrects a name in the earlier continuation study: selector 100 (`0x64`) is
`FlushCache`, while `sceSifStopDma` is selector 107 (`0x6B`). The original
`2DC120` export was already named `ps2KernelFlushCache`; its raw instructions
and syscall contract were correct. The pinned
[PS2SDK syscall definitions](https://github.com/ps2dev/ps2sdk/blob/57d60f4bbde84697652cf316276a3564db549d11/ee/kernel/include/syscallnr.h)
and SIF initialization source corroborate the cache flush before DMAC access.
Unity's HD AssetBundle handling supplies context for asset loading, but does
not supply the original PS2 kernel/SIF initialization implementation.

Three more complete native variants (`2DFAD8`, `2DE508`, `2DDE38`) recover 379
instructions and 1,516 bytes, retaining their 112/64/96-byte frames and later
branches. A separate original four-instruction `2DC120` leaf routes the real
bank values through the strict kernel bridge. The existing movie-family cache
leaf uses a different external callback and cannot be substituted into this
worker proof. Strict compilation and independent source review pass. These
four variants were already registered; they add no identity credit.

The new OS world explicitly chooses SDK semaphore 25 with maximum/initial
counts 1 and option 0, preserving the actual packet's unwritten words. It
requires an accepted worker Wait24, rather than treating the worker's initial
count 0 as evidence of execution. Original and C independently evaluate the
new requests. Existing default OS modes stay separate; the new C observation
has a 348-byte interface containing a 316-byte policy instead of projecting
it into the old 296-byte interface. Pure model and transport tests cover
deferred creation, rejected packets, reentry, unchanged failed observations,
and the combined request budget.

The [original startup capture](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-startup-results-independent-review.json)
now executes 573 worker instructions from the same genuine bootstrap input.
The real SDK routine publishes the returned semaphore ID at `52C344` and
acquires it. Original SIF initialization writes its flags at `52C2BC` and
`52C2B8`; the file-RPC ready flag at `52C340` remains zero. The worker stops at
`2DC124` before the unadmitted `FlushCache(0)` reply, with fourteen accepted
records. The actual cold semaphore packet was detached at its CreateSema
boundary because later native frames reuse that stack address. The new
minimum worker SP is `C43D70`. The first MMIO read at `2DDF7C` has not executed.

The [C comparison](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-startup-C-validation.json)
passes the same boundary on the isolated 546-unit module (541 reused objects,
five local compiles, 4,454 exports). All 9,736,192 RAM bytes, all 304 raw bank
elements, both complete stacks (24 KiB), fourteen accepted records, full V1
values, ordered primitive/syscall traces, indirect events, context/Status
metadata, the detached SDK packet and native global publications agree.
The real failed fifteenth request is recorded without response or model
commit. This remains a chosen OS test world, not live PCSX2 or hardware proof;
cache/device effects, RPC arrival, file completion and the foreground pump
remain unproved. No checkpoint, backup tree or copied input vector was made.
The [two retained comparisons](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-startup-retained-regressions.json)
pass on the new module: the earlier twelve-record worker case still stops at
the old cold mutex boundary in the original OS mode, and positive loaded-model
case 7 still completes. The new OS mode is selected explicitly. These checks
compare 19,451,904 RAM bytes, 32,768 stack bytes and 118 records. They do not
replace or claim a rerun of the previous full 37-case result.

The [three-body DDS1 transfer scan](battle-action-skill/sif-startup-DDS-transfer-leads.json)
finds no exact or calls-only matches in the declared DDS1 code sections.
Only actual J/JAL destination bits may vary; stack offsets, relative branches,
constants and whole-span bytes remain exact. This bounded negative result
does not establish that DDS lacks equivalent SDK behavior.

The [82-function DDS scan](battle-action-skill-DDS-transfer-leads.json) finds
five unique exact whole-span matches and fourteen unique calls-only matches,
with the exact matches included in the fourteen. Ordinary instruction bits,
branch displacements and stack/field offsets are not normalized. Thirty direct
call pairs are consistent. One candidate is ambiguous, three short spans were
excluded, and 64 have no calls-only match. These are bounded transfer leads;
DDS function identities and runtime behavior remain separate work.

The [43-function libc transfer scan](battle-action-skill/libc-DDS-transfer-leads.json)
finds 29 unique calls-only whole-span candidates in DDS1, including 14 exact
matches. All 36 direct-call target pairs are consistent. The Cvt root has a
unique candidate; dtoa has no match under this deliberately narrow comparison.
Only real J/JAL destination fields are ignored; all stack offsets, relative
branches, constants and other instruction bits remain exact. These findings
support shared-library transfer work without assigning DDS names or recovery
credit from patterns alone.

## Runtime checked cache and first hardware-read boundary

The [cache runtime observation](runtime-cache-startup-observation.json) now
records a real hidden PCSX2 cold boot through the original `FlushCache(0)` call
at `2DC124`, returning to `2DDF74`. Loaded leaf and caller bytes match our ELF.
The installed BIOS changes V0 to 2, V1 to `190`, T0 to `1000` and T1 to 0.
Its loaded syscall table and both inspected code blocks match unique bytes in
the installed BIOS. The mode-0 cache loop and dispatcher explain these values;
they are not inferred from the SDK's void prototype. Scalar instructions retain
the upper 64-bit lanes. The [independent review](battle-action-skill/cache-boundary-runtime-independent-review.json)
binds the observations, native words, BIOS, ELF and explicit reply configuration.

Actual guest loads observed `D_STAT = 00800040` at `2DDF7C` and
`D5_CHCR = 00000184` at `2DDF9C`. The low `20` acknowledgement branch was not
taken. No debugger read of a hardware-register window supplied these values.
These are observations from one installed emulator/BIOS boot, not default
device inputs or evidence of file completion. All owned probes were removed
and the owned process exited; no guest writes or savestates were used.

The [pinned community SDK and emulator study](battle-action-skill/sif-mmio-startup-study.json)
corroborates the D_STAT write-one-to-clear flags and D5 channel STR test. If STR
is clear the next kernel boundary is SetDChain selector 120; if set it is
AddDmacHandler selector 18 with arguments `[5, 2DE330, 0]`. Both routes require
two more complete four-instruction leaves beyond the current 1,017-body
original availability set. Registering `2DE330` does not execute that callback.
The HD AssetBundle path does not supply this PS2 kernel/device implementation.

The new cache response is an explicit external test-world input with coherent
flat RAM. It models only the reviewed low-lane outputs and preserves the other
program banks. It does not emulate the BIOS's privileged scratch registers,
CP0 Count/EPC changes, real cache coherence or timing. Those distinctions must
survive transfer to DDS and other SMT PS2 builds. Hardware-register values and
DMA/RPC completion remain separate external events requiring their own model.

The [fresh original capture](battle-action-skill/worker-mmio-read-results-independent-review.json)
now completes 576 worker instructions (3,559 total), accepting fifteen OS calls,
then rejects the actual four-byte read at `2DDF7C`. All state was detached
before attempting that instruction; its failed execution context is never
queried or resumed. The memory bank returns neither a pointer nor a value for
the device access. The same original bootstrap input row is used unchanged.

The [native C comparison](battle-action-skill/worker-mmio-read-C-validation.json)
passes on the 547-unit probe, using 544 unchanged objects and three local
compiles. The existing whole SIF bodies differ only by the linked address
resolver hook; their COFF code and relocations have an exact alias inverse.
All 304 raw bank elements, 9,736,192 RAM bytes, both raw stacks (24 KiB), fifteen
accepted records, full V1 values, syscall/primitive ordering, indirect events,
thread context/Status metadata, SDK publications and the rejected MMIO request
agree. The original cache-effect receipt is an additional diagnostic; its
resulting banks are compared rather than claiming a separate C effect trace.

All [three retained cases](battle-action-skill/worker-mmio-read-retained-regressions.json)
pass on that module: the earlier twelve-record worker, fourteen-record SDK/SIF
startup and positive model case 7. Their original OS modes remain explicit;
the new cache world is not enabled implicitly. This is a selected regression,
not a rerun of the full 37-case set. Published integration remains 2,593 of
9,060 functions; this step validates previously recovered startup code and adds
no new function identity. Device responses, DMA interrupt delivery, RPC/file
completion, all 2,624 family cases and canonical integration remain pending.

The [four-state device configuration](battle-action-skill/sif-dmac-device-initial-state.json)

and [transport tests](battle-action-skill/sif-dmac-C-transport-tests.json) cover

Two complete four-instruction syscall leaves, `2DBBB0` and `2DC280`, are now

instruction set to 1,019 whole bodies / 46,318 instructions. These leaves were
already registered and add no new identity credit. The three existing SIF
bodies retain all 379 original operation statements; their word-access helpers
now call explicit scalar device hooks. That helper change changes native host
code and is not merely a relocation substitution. No RAM page or pointer is
manufactured for hardware registers.

The [fresh original runs](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-dmac-run-review.json)
use the same unmodified bootstrap input row for all four states. The worker
completes 587, 590, 585 and 588 instructions respectively, with fifteen accepted

STR set reaches `AddDmacHandler(5, 2DE330, 0)` at `2DBBB4`; STR clear reaches
`SetDChain()` at `2DC284`. Both calls stop before any response. Registering the
callback remains separate from executing it.

The [C comparison](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-dmac-C-validation.json) passes
all four states on the 550-unit module: 544 reused objects, six local compiles
and 4,465 exports. Each case compares all 304 raw bank elements, 9,736,192 RAM
bytes, both complete stacks, the fifteen OS records, full V1 values, ordered
syscall/primitive traces, indirect calls, context/Status metadata and the exact
device state and events. Device event PCs in C are attributed by the unique

syscall; failed execution state is never inspected or resumed.

The [four retained comparisons](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-dmac-retained-regressions.json)

their original OS modes. The full 37-case suite was not rerun.

The [new live observation](runtime-dmac-startup-observation.json) also follows
the actual installed BIOS through the channel-5 handler registration. Its
return changes AT, A0, A1, A2, T0, HI and LO as well as V0/V1. The loaded
seven-instruction wrapper matches unique BIOS bytes and calls `80001B10`, but
does not establish that deeper helper's full semantics. Consequently the
original/C test world still provides neither selector-18 nor selector-120
responses. This ABI and device distinction matters when transferring SDK
findings to DDS or other SMT games; HD AssetBundle behavior cannot establish

RPC/file completion and foreground resumption remain the next recovery work.

## Handler allocation inputs and further native SIF startup bodies

The [new kernel observation](runtime-sif-kernel-startup-observation.json)
follows actual registration, interrupt enabling and the first SIF register
query. The [independent review](battle-action-skill/sif-kernel-runtime-independent-review.json)
matches all 848 unique displayed instruction words exactly to the installed
BIOS image. Named registration records occupy only 56 unique bytes before
and after the call. The allocator removes a free-list node, inserts it into
channel 5's list and increments the counts. It stores the actual handler,
GP and A3 values in the node. Thus the SDK's three formal arguments do not
describe every live input consumed by this installed BIOS implementation.

The [explicit initial input](battle-action-skill/sif-kernel-registration-initial-state.json)
contains fourteen pre-call kernel words only. It contains no handler ID or
post-call CPU outputs. The returned ID must be calculated from the selected
node and the original multiply/shift sequence; the live ID of 2 is not a
universal reply. The [input extractor](battle-action-skill/sif-kernel-registration-input-review.json)
also verifies that removing all return observations and the after-call table
does not affect this input configuration.

The [BIOS state study](battle-action-skill/kernel-registration-state-study.json)
computes all observed after-call table bytes from those inputs using twelve
original word stores. It also calculates the returned index and HI/LO values
from the signed R5900 multiply with its explicit destination register, followed
by the arithmetic shift. This is not an MFHI-based division. The same study
records SetDChain's ordered CHCR/QWC writes and readback, including its separate
exception-Status effect on AT. These source-derived transitions are ready for
the next model; they have not yet supplied responses to original/C execution.

The loaded `EnableDmac` implementation returns 0 if the mask is already
enabled, executing a taken branch-likely delay that clears V0; otherwise it
writes the mask and returns 1. The live input/output V0 values both being 1
does not establish preservation. The first GetReg call reads software table
entry `8001E080`, directly observed as `19500` before and after. This value
selects the original nonzero branch on this boot. It is runtime kernel state,
not a constant in ROM or evidence of a completed file request.

Four more [complete native variants](battle-action-skill-sif-os-startup-frames-source-review.json)
now cover `2DBC00`, `2DC2A0`, `2DC2B0` and `2DCAB8`: 38 instructions and 152
bytes. The whole 48-byte wrapper retains its Status-bit test, actual DI/EI
helpers, mandatory `SYNC 0`, saved registers and untouched frame bytes.
Both strict C compilation profiles pass the temporary-bank tests; the
[independent source review](battle-action-skill-sif-os-startup-frames-independent-review.json)
checks the exact original words and raw interfaces. At that source-only stage,
the availability projection covered 1,023 bodies / 46,356 instructions;
original/C device results still stopped at selector 18/120. The following
integration now exercises the registration path in a separate module.

These bodies were already registered, so published recovery credit is
unchanged. That integration must compute selector-18/120 state and CPU
effects from inputs and accept the exact `SYNC 0` site at `2DCAF4`. Correction:
the active `BattleActionSkillKernelValueCallbacks` owns SYNC and historically
accepted arbitrary subtypes; `PoolValueCallbacks` has a subtype-16 guard but
supplies only `_plzcw` in this driver. The new bounded projection permits 16
and permits 0 only at `2DCAF4` with original word `0000000F`. The
[provider tests](battle-action-skill/sif-os-sync-provider-projection-tests.json)
exercise the actual projected callback. Actual callbacks, DMA arrivals,
RPC/file completion and all 2,624 family cases remain separate work. The
kernel list layout and raw-call conventions are useful SMT SDK transfer
leads, requiring a fresh per-build check before applying them to DDS.

The [new original capture](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-kernel-run-review.json)

cover publication ordering, rejected/stale requests and retained modes.
Primary HI/LO publication uses the existing shared-bank setter, preserving

diagnostics; C compares the computed receipt and all final banks without

The new wrapper has executed its saves and DI before the current selector-22
stop. Its later `SYNC 0` remains covered by callback tests only until that

The [next continuation study](battle-action-skill/enable-getreg-continuation-study.json)
