## Interrupt enable, software register and native DMA submission prefix

The [ready/cache capture](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-ready-run-review.json)
and [557-unit C comparison](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-ready-C-validation.json)
now pass all four fresh paths through selector 22, the negative software-index
branch of selector 122, and the native command/cache/descriptor code. They stop
at the unprovided selector 119 call, `2DC264`. All 304 bank elements, nine RAM
spans, complete 8 KiB foreground and 16 KiB worker stacks, 74 accepted records,
14 computed kernel effects and four cache receipts agree. The four originals
complete 3,709 / 3,712 / 3,714 / 3,717 instructions including their prefixes.

The [new policy tests](battle-action-skill/sif-ready-kernel-world-model-tests.json)
cover both interrupt-mask branches and the software-word zero/sign boundaries.
EnableDmac reads current D_STAT and conditionally toggles its channel-5 enable
bit. GetReg reads the [explicit pre-call word](battle-action-skill/sif-ready-input-review.json)
at `8001E080`, preserving LW sign extension and the actual V1 upper lane. Its
observed value is an input for this boot domain, not a fixed service return.
The zero-word readiness-polling branch remains a separate execution domain.

The [whole cache helper](battle-action-skill-sif-cache-frames-source-review.json)
retains all 43 instructions, eleven SYNC sites and nine CACHE sites. Its
effective cache addresses follow the actual SLA's 32-bit OFF_BASE operand while
temporary register arithmetic keeps the original low64 behavior. The coherent
flat-RAM world records each operation and its exact placement between primitives;
it does not model physical dirty cache lines or cache timing. The
[three native command bodies](battle-action-skill-sif-command-frames-source-review.json)
add 96 instructions for `2DE2B0`, `2DE178` and `2DC260`, preserving 16-/128-byte
guest frames and actual packet/descriptor stores. All four paths produce a
20-byte command packet and the descriptor `[0059BA80, 00019500, 00000014, 00000044]`
in hex.
Neither is seeded from an AFTER capture.

The [independent source review](battle-action-skill/sif-ready-integration-source-independent-review.json)
and [actual module review](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-ready-probe-independent-review.json)
check those native statements, raw interfaces and single shared bank ownership.
An actual capture attempt exposed an inherited stop-tag mismatch at selector119;
the callback tag was corrected, then all four originals were rerun fresh. The
current passing outputs replace the failed attempt. The C module was unchanged
by that diagnostic correction. No failed emulator context was resumed.

All [twelve retained scenarios](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-ready-retained-regressions.json)
also pass on this module, including the four previous registration paths with
their original modes. Their totals are 116,813,824 RAM bytes, 278,528 stack
bytes and 273 records. The [stored results review](battle-action-skill/worker-sif-ready-C-results-independent-review.json)
keeps those targeted regressions separate from full 37-case or 2,624-case coverage.

The [installed-BIOS selector119 study](battle-action-skill/sif-setdma119-static-study.json)
identifies the next required state: DMA channel 6, its kernel ring and global
DMA-enable registers. A 20-byte transfer reads two full quadwords, so its input
footprint is 32 bytes. An LQ into V0 can also change V0's upper64; a model of
scalar returns alone would lose that effect. A subsequent
[live hidden-boot observation](runtime-sif-setdma119-observation.json) binds all
335 loaded words exactly to that BIOS image and the loaded leaf/caller to the
original ELF. It reads only the actual descriptor, 32 source bytes and 164 bytes
of selected ring state per boundary, with no debugger MMIO reads or guest writes.
The observed ring index advances from 6 to 7 and V0's low64 becomes `701` in hex;
its upper64 becomes `2059BA402059B9C0`, exactly source bytes +24..31. These are
observations to explain and recompute, not constants admitted to the C model.
All owned probes were removed and the owned emulator exited. Actual channel-6
device inputs/evolution, DMA completion, IOP arrival, callbacks and foreground
resumption remain unproved. Published
integration remains 2,593 functions; these SDK identities were already registered.

The [independent runtime review](battle-action-skill/sif-setdma119-runtime-independent-review.json)
passes 670 checks, including the rounded source copy and complete displayed
register differences. The live boot's SP `01FFEC80` differs from the comparison
worker's `00C43D40`; this does not establish live thread 41 or identical kernel
state at worker startup.

The [whole-program lead ranking](next-breadth-recovery-leads.json) identifies
27 unregistered bodies outside the active battle family. The strongest next
group is twelve recovery calculators/applications, 209 original instructions,
with Unity HD roles and two already registered dependencies. Negotiation Hojo

## Recovery-service calculators and application

The twelve recovery-service bodies at `0020DBF0` through `0020DEB0` are now
implemented in [recovery_service.c](../src/recovery_service.c). Their twelve
Unity HD `fclRecoverCalc` roles are recorded in the
[interface and input review](recovery-service-preflight.json). Existing pinned
healing/recruitment research supplies context; no direct community mapping of
this class was found. Original instructions establish the algorithms and the
236-byte PS2 unit layout; managed HD layouts and modified healing policies are
kept separate.

[1,664 original-instruction cases](recovery-service-original.json), covering
954,186 steps, pass against both the small probe and the
[525-unit main build](recovery-service-main-C-validation.json). These include
all 256 low-byte service selectors, all 32 combinations of the five relevant
status bits, actual player/enemy passive-skill paths, signed parameter clamps,
HP/MP deficits and excess, and 32 connected eight-call sequences. The real
maximum-HP/MP dependencies execute; cached maxima and captured outputs do not
supply their answers. All declared ordinary RAM, raw V0 results, Status, the
FPU value profile and primitive counts agree. The original runner checks native
callee saves. The [independent results review](recovery-service-results-review.json)
records the current main proof and the superseded probe-host epoch separately.

Important PS2 details include unsigned MP-delta conversion before multiplying
by 2.5, signed HP-delta conversion, and wrapping word additions. Excess MP can
therefore produce a saturated positive price. The dispel-price routine returns
400 in its actual JR delay slot; older pseudocode incorrectly showed zero.
Application functions retain their defined PS2 V0 values even where HD declares
`void`. Full recovery executes revive, HP/MP restoration and status clearing in
that order, retaining status bit `8000`; dispelling is a separate operation.

All twelve [native interfaces](recovery-service-native-ghidra.json) were saved,
reopened and exported with original instruction bytes. Full volatile banks,
guest save frames, HI/LO, arithmetic FCSR flags and physical gameplay remain
outside this semantic proof. These layouts, masks and call patterns are useful
SMT search leads; each related game's executable must establish its own prices,
unit layout, flags and ABI.

The [publication](recovery-service-publication.json) registers exactly these
twelve new identities, taking the current union to 2,605/9,060 functions and
432,544 recognized body bytes. The unchanged 413-function/462,684-case broad
suite also passes against the 525-unit main build. The remaining fifteen
ranked candidates receive no credit yet.

The next [Hojo work study](hojo-work-next-study.json) closes its nine-word jump
table to three local targets and checks all 370 selected instruction words.
Its full64 type comparisons, uncapped initializer appends, pairwise swap order,
and search-only support for skills `01A0`/`01A1` are input and implementation
obligations for the next recovery. The original skill records exclude those
two IDs from newly initialized work despite permitting them in existing work.

## Negotiation Hojo work and fusion accidents

The [Hojo source review](negotiation-hojo-source-review.json) and
[fusion source review](fusion-accident-source-review.json) cover ten complete
bodies, 594 original instructions and 2,376 recognized bytes. Readable Unity
roles guide identification, while original instructions determine layouts,
arithmetic, call order and returns. Pinned randomizer
[notes](https://github.com/nmarkro/Nocturne-Randomizer/blob/f74e85c0f632b751eaf278046884961c618c8541/notes/nocturne_random.txt)
give recruitment/fusion context and tentative table leads. The native tables
were checked independently; uncertain file/RAM offsets were not imported.

The original lead for `001FD3B0` was wrong: it implements the 34-row
event-eligibility scan, matching `fclCombineCalcCore.cmbChkEventDevil` at
`18240F410`. The previously suggested `fclCombineDisableCmdZensyo` UI helper
has different inputs and effects. The corrected mapping is recorded in the
fusion review rather than treating a matching-looking name as proof.

[1,393 original cases](negotiation-fusion-original.json), over 3,375,374 steps,
pass against the probe and [527-unit main build](negotiation-fusion-main-C-validation.json).
Sixty-four connected Hojo sequences add 1,088 actual calls per build. Each
build compares 91,319,508 mutable RAM bytes and checks 138,837,524 immutable
table bytes. The original driver checks loaded table hashes and native callee
saves; the C runs use real existing lookup, parameter, event, default-unit and
RNG dependencies. Their host profile and global bindings are restored afterward.
The [input review](negotiation-hojo-input-profile-review.json) checks the graph
and meaningful tie controls; the [stored results review](negotiation-fusion-results-review.json)
keeps earlier probe and current main epochs distinct.

Hojo clears fourteen 128-byte formations but populates only the first four.
It appends without an eight-skill cap; backed cases observe 9-, 12- and
24-match writes into the following formation. Its pairwise parameter-4 swaps
can reverse ties, and the tested used/empty leading candidate makes that order
observable. Type comparisons use all 64 bits. Original kind/family records
exclude `01A0`/`01A1` during initialization, while direct type-3 search accepts
them in existing work. RNG selection preserves duplicate slots and marks every
matching slot, followed by the formation-used flag.

Fusion's type routine always returns zero but still advances RNG. Meta-only
eligibility returns the raw mask value 2. The selector retains all sixteen
attempts and actual event/race/level/stock/meta/excluded-ID filters. The reverse
fourteen-entry fallback returns entry zero even when all entries are occupied
and it equals the excluded ID. A [separate focused original/C case](negotiation-fusion-fallback-exhaustion.json)
reaches the otherwise uncovered exhausted-scan instruction and confirms return
59 with all fourteen IDs occupied. Stat boosts preserve signed-byte division
by five, byte wrapping and real HP/MP recomputation.

All ten [native interfaces](negotiation-fusion-native-ghidra.json) were saved,
reopened and exported. The [retained twelve-function recovery replay](negotiation-fusion-focused-regressions.json)
passes all 1,664 cases on the current main build. All prior 525 object files
are unchanged. The full 413-function/462,684-case regression belongs to the
preceding 525-unit epoch; it was not rerun for these two additive source units.
Full volatile banks, private guest frames, HI/LO, arithmetic FCSR flags and
physical gameplay remain outside these semantic proofs. The two new shared
SMT fingerprints require per-game layout/table/ABI checks before transfer.

The [ten-routine publication](negotiation-fusion-publication.json) added exactly
ten new identities: its then-current integrated union was **2,615/9,060 functions**,
with 434,920 recognized bytes and 527 source units. At that publication, five entries
in the earlier breadth ranking remained unregistered; no dependency or
supplemental edge-case identity is counted again.

The [next five-function study](evolution-negotiation-next-study.json) verifies
487 original instructions and their existing dependency exports. Evolution's
null-output path does not define a universal selected-ID V0 result. Flow6
queue counts are signed and can be negative; queued script IDs are not script
execution. The item/Hearts branch needs the first skill-level view at `339E1A`,
not the older harness binding at `333CF8`, and global-work backing through
`+11D30`. These are requirements for new fixtures, not changes to earlier
proofs or additional recovery credit.

## Native action callback integration and SMT transfer leads

The current [route audit](battle-callback-route-audit.json),
[native frame study](battle-callback-frame-study.json), and
[independent parent source review](battle-callback-native-independent-source-review.json)
establish the retail callback structure from original instructions. The observed
table prefix at PS2 RAM `0x003FA7C0` contains 23 pairs of 32-bit entry/update
addresses, eight bytes per state. This is an observed prefix, not a proven full
table extent. The native signed-byte index is unchecked; the four zero cells
are not evidence of supported null callbacks.

The action context stores flags at `+0x04`, current state at `+0x08`, pending
state at `+0x09`, and a saturating unsigned 16-bit timer at `+0x0A`. Flag `0x02`
requests deferred entry, `0x40` suppresses dispatch, and `0x400` invokes stock
selection before the remaining process logic. Main's `+0xE4` cursor selects the
preceding member of the four process slots at `+0xD4`; negative subtraction
wraps to slot three. These context fields are distinct from the negotiation
result at Main `+0x94` and the separate context selector at `+0x68`.

The complete SetSeq/Process parents have 16/32-byte native frames. Their nested
call therefore enters SetSeq 48 bytes below the incoming Process stack pointer.
The inner callback must inherit Process's actual local S0/context and S1/one,
while preserving the other incoming bank lanes. This is a useful cross-title
search pattern: paired callback table, signed-byte state lookup, deferred-entry
flag, preceding-process ring and timer saturation. It remains a transfer lead;
addresses, field offsets, callback identities and frame sizes must be verified
against each other SMT executable. HD delegate names accelerate discovery but
do not establish the PS2 frame or register contract.

The native parent source is emitted and strictly object-compiled. The
[authored input cohort](battle-callback-inputs.json) contains 1,088 initial worlds
and 1,472 parent calls, including 224 connected cases. The initial 1,024-row
prefix is retained exactly; 64 supplemental worlds set the original UnitWork
action type to 10 to exercise the transitive Choice call. The
[original-instruction run](battle-callback-original.json)
passes over 1,649,657 steps, observing 1,324 actual callback calls and 300 nested
Process entries, including 64 actual calls at `00257130`.
The isolated C candidate links five source units, including ten whole Choice
bodies and the corrected Outcome-to-Choice bank handoff at `00257130`.
The first C comparison passed 194 completed cases before a native process
assertion in a nested transition to READY. The
[private-child debugger observation](battle-callback-native-abort-debug.json)
traced native printf's `40/10` conversion to `002E65C8`: C RAM at `0054C078`
contained zeroes, while the original 256-byte bit-length table has entry 10
equal to four. The original table SHA-256 is
`14a5d850c255623f9472e3c650abce0c78d32f0276b315b3a276a0462d97a1ac`.
The [original ELF input initializer](original-ELF-input-image.json) supplies the

The [HD expression comparison](battle-panel-color-native-HD-comparison.json)
finds all 768 recorded native returns agree with the retained
`effMath$$effColorMultiply` integer expression. Each packed channel computes
`((first * second) >> 7) & 255`; 255 multiplied by 255 produces 508 before
byte packing, yielding 252. The native search fingerprint is paired
PEXTLB/PEXTLH zero extension, QMTC2/VITOF0, multiplication by 1/128, component
multiplication, multiplication by 128, VFTOI0 and PPACH/PPACB. The two static
integer expressions also agree over all 65,536 byte pairs; this does not add
native execution coverage. The HD counterpart identifies the arithmetic role,
while PS2 upper-register preservation and VF2 scratch effects require original
instructions. Every related SMT build needs independent matching and checking.

The [whole caller study](battle-panel-color-caller-study.json) now identifies six
direct color callers (2,195 instructions) in the StatusPairs renderer closure.
Their complete native-bank source compiles, with 21 external native-call routes
and the finite ordering-table callback still awaiting genuine providers. The
bounded closure has 162 bodies / 12,526 instructions after including the real
ordering-table initializer and its known callback. Eight bodies write full128
GPRs: the color leaf, PartyDraw, memcpy, memmove, memset, strlen, formatter and
memchr. For example, native strlen uses PAND on A0 at `002D5D94`; preserving
upper lanes blindly across a semantic string call would lose a real writer.
This is a transfer lead about EE register liveness, not another title
validation. Original `002ACDC0` initializes the ordering-table callback at
record+16 to `002ACD40`; the on-disc word at `002F6808` is zero before that
initializer executes. A static zero table word must not be mistaken for a
callable initialized runtime record.

The complete six-caller source passes independent review of all 2,195
instructions. Another 73 complete prerequisite bodies (3,184 instructions)
compile in debug and optimized builds, including the two interrupt leaves.
These are source and compiler results; caller execution and genuine routing
through libc, font, ordering-table and interrupt dependencies remain pending.

now compiles in both modes. Its scalar writes preserve the actual upper64 and

The first original capture stopped after 258 completed worlds and 390 calls.
The [input-layout diagnosis](battle-status-pairs-input-layout-gap.json) traces
the failure to an overlapping process/Kouka object and a missing font style
selected by the original MSG1 control bytes. The repair also gives message
handles valid allocator-owned metadata slots outside their payloads. These
are input-world defects; the failed capture does not validate the full cohort.
After those repairs, the next original run completes 512 worlds and 768 calls
before reaching unsupported `SYNC 0` at `002DC840`, immediately before index
cache operation `002DC844`. The [cache-boundary study](battle-status-pairs-cache-boundary-research.json)
checks the pinned PCSX2 implementation. Its SYNC value operation is empty;
its [actual cache implementation](https://github.com/PCSX2/pcsx2/blob/b4d6e2aa7b9069162fa0e7cbc45ae95fc2566f39/pcsx2/Cache.cpp)
does implement DXLTG/DXIN, and DXLTG publishes flags to CP0 TagLo. The old SYNC
comment about unimplemented cache behavior must not be used as a blanket
cache-no-op model. This remains a backend boundary, with no supplied cache tags
or function result.
The scoped SYNC0/16 callback subsequently passed an actual instruction probe.
Ghidra keeps the first duplicate registration, so replacement explicitly
unregisters it first. The latest [original capture](battle-status-pairs-original.json)
now stops cleanly before `CACHE` at `002DC844`: actual child index 1,
RA `002DC924`, SP `0064EE10`, Status/FCSR zero. The 512 completed worlds and
768 calls remain a partial prefix of the requested 1,024/1,536 cohort.

The [cache platform study](status-pairs-cache-platform-study.json) distinguishes
the EE manual's full physical TagLo fields from the pinned PCSX2 implementation.
The manual defines physical tag bits 31:12 and D/V/LRF/L bits 6:3; indexed
DXLTG reads those fields without writing dirty data back. The pinned emulator
instead exposes flags and performs a dirty writeback during DXLTG. Earlier live
TagLo-zero observations do not distinguish these behaviors. Recovery therefore
keeps explicit, separate hardware-field and emulator profiles, each starting
from authored indexed line state rather than a sequence of supplied TagLo
answers. Ordinary flat-RAM accesses still do not establish hardware cache fills,
coherence or timing. The 15-unit native comparison used an explicit diagnostic
mode for the contiguous completed original prefix; it did not certify the full
family or add function identities. The new cache epoch requires a fresh full
cohort and rejects rerunning that historical prefix through its new module.
The [installed-runtime observation](runtime-indexed-cache-independent-review.json)
subsequently caught seven actual boundaries through both first-set DXLTG/MFC0
pairs and the native scan return. Both TagLo values were zero, so the observation
does not select a cache profile. Loaded SDK bytes match the original executable;
the owned probes were removed and the hidden emulator process exited. No dirty
writeback, indexed invalidation or internal cache-line state was observed.

throws before uninitialized bytes can be consumed; returning false alone is
insufficient in the installed Ghidra implementation.

This missing block is original ELF BSS, not cache data. Native `002C13D8`
initializes seven 8-byte bank records at `00593A10` with signed ID `-1` and bytes

It does not manufacture a ready bank, IOP arrival or thread identifier.
The [repaired original capture](battle-status-pairs-original.json) reaches the
actual `WakeupThread` syscall at `002DBDD4` with no uninitialized reads and no
tainted boundary. This path has queued the missing-bank filename rather than
reading a manufactured resident bank. Its cache generation is 151; `A0=0`,
`RA=002C1210` and `SP=0064EE30`. No syscall return has been supplied.

The [read-guard instruction test](battle-status-pairs-unbacked-read-microprobe.json)
executes the original faulting LW and a real executed delay-slot load. Both
stop before an unbacked value reaches the destination. A backed load consumes
its authored value, and an annulled branch-likely delay does not read or fault.

The downstream selector is **WakeupThread `0x33` (51)**. It is distinct from
SignalSema `0x42` (66), as corroborated by the pinned
[PS2SDK syscall definitions](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ps2dev/ps2sdk/57d60f4bbde84697652cf316276a3564db549d11/ee/kernel/include/syscallnr.h).
Native `002C0FF8` creates the worker using entry `002C0FC0`, stack
`00591840`/`0x1000`, GP `00554170` and priority `0x42`, then stores the actual
CreateThread result in `00552138`. Existing semaphore count models cannot
substitute for that scheduler operation. The existing
[sound transfer study](sound-bank-flow-transfer.json) retains the complete
Play-body DDS counterpart as static evidence; the HD managed sound path provides
role context rather than the PS2 queue and kernel implementation.

One [hidden runtime observation](runtime-wakeup-thread-observation.json) captures
the actual WakeupThread syscall, next instruction and leaf return during natural
boot. Its input is thread ID 3, with `RA=002DCC40` and `SP=0059B0D0`;
`V0` returns 3 and `V1` changes from `0x33` to `0xCC`. The loaded syscall slot
`8001204C` points to `80003340`, and the captured handler words match the installed
BIOS. All six available register categories were retained; this is not proof
for unobserved banks or arbitrary thread states. Owned probes were removed,
execution resumed, the owned process exited, and configuration bytes remained
unchanged. This observation does not supply a response for the StatusPairs
sound path's independently authored thread-ID-zero state.

The [installed BIOS study](battle-wakeup-kernel-study.json) follows the actual
wrapper into static scheduler code. ID zero resolves through the current-thread
global at `800125EC`; it is not intrinsically an invalid ID. A running row
(`status=1`) then takes the failure branch, while ready/sleep/suspended states
have distinct wake-count and queue effects. The table uses `0x4C`-byte rows at
`80017408`. Caller `V0` also depends on saved-context restoration: the wrapper
saves raw `A0` as the initial result before invoking the helper, and failure
patches only its lower 64 bits. Thus neither the observed ID-3 result nor the
helper's normalized ID defines the return for arbitrary callers. Deeper helper
bytes and scheduler RAM were not observed live. These are version-specific
platform leads, without Nocturne function-identity credit.

The [initial current-16 failure study](battle-status-pairs-hilo-failure-study.json)
found exact equality at the cold syscall for all recorded banks, cache lines,
17 ordinary RAM extents, the full stack, immutable strings and call progress.
The overall first replay failed: 141 of the 512 returned worlds disagreed only
in primary HI/LO. Their independently authored dirty upper words expose the
active eecore three-operand MULT definition's partial publication. This is an
original-instruction backend investigation, not justification to patch C's
post-call registers or accept the overall diagnostic. The existing scoped
`ParticleColourMultValues.java` documents the same language defect in two
other instructions. Any extension needs original opcode membership, processor
semantics and instruction-level validation before the family is recaptured.

The [actual instruction probe](battle-status-pairs-multiply-microprobe.json)
now confirms the compiled-language defect directly: its selected MULT and
MULT1 operations multiply two 4-byte inputs into a 4-byte temporary before
widening. The scoped correction passes 248 instruction cases, including eight
exact JAL/delay cases; 269 untouched multiply/divide cases also pass. It replaces
full 64-bit product halves as specified, preserves unrelated banks and GPR
upper halves, and rejects hardware-undefined non-word operands before writing.
The [independent review](battle-status-pairs-multiply-independent-review.json)
binds the actual workspace language and distinguishes it from the installed
extension's different sources. No installed language, C source or native binary
has changed. The [locality review](battle-status-pairs-multiply-locality-review.json)
reconstructs the previous original wire exactly by reverting only those 141
special-register arrays; all other bytes and step counts are unchanged.

Before the all-world extension, the 16-unit diagnostic passed all 512 returned
worlds and 769 completed calls, then matched the
770th attempted call at the same genuine WakeupThread boundary. It compares
904,267,665 ordinary RAM bytes across completed worlds and the boundary,
plus the boundary's full 131,072-byte stack and 31 immutable bytes. Complete
CPU/FPR/VU values, all four HI/LO owners, cache lines/TagLo/generation,
counters, sort receipts and typed call progress match. The tainted native
observer is sampled and its worker exits; it is never resumed or reset.
No kernel response or result for the stopped child is supplied. The
[stored-results review](battle-status-pairs-native-boundary-result-review.json)

The [all-world original diagnostic](battle-status-pairs-original.json) visits
all 1,024 authored worlds in fresh script/ProgramDB/emulator contexts. Its first

The failed worlds are retained as failures, never normalized into external

The [COMM descriptor study](battle-status-pairs-COMM-descriptor-study.json)
distinguishes two unrelated fields at offset `4C`: action Context+4C is a scalar
message/packet argument, while COMM userdata+4C is a party-descriptor pointer.
Native `25D500` stores its incoming descriptor there; `25D2A0` passes it to
`2469C0`, which reads flags at +0 and signed index at +4. The existing FORM
descriptor already resolves to the correct UnitWork in every authored world.
Replacing the accidentally authored scalar in COMM preserves the party-index
axis without backing low addresses or supplying a lookup result.

The [passive original stock trace](battle-status-pairs-stock-fault-study.json)

Main+128 reference and passes zero through `279A88` to the texture getter.
The [source-bound repair study](battle-status-pairs-stock-texture-BEFORE-study.json)
checks native initializer `279708`: it establishes four texture references at
Main+120/+124/+128/+12C. The warm input graph had supplied only the first two.
Completing the same preexisting descriptor graph needs no new RAM extent,
renderer replacement, GS-residency claim or fabricated kernel/IOP response.

Both repairs are now emitted and independently checked. The actual new wire is
`cd8249d4...`; undoing only those changes reconstructs `27b649bf...` exactly,
then the earlier sound/cache inverses still reconstruct their prior inputs.
The next full original run records **725 returned worlds, 65 clean syscall
stops and 234 failed worlds**. The former COMM and null-texture faults are gone.
All eight roots and 1,536 planned call positions remain present. Call progress
for failed worlds is not invented. The new all-world C controller is implemented
and source/mock reviewed; it has not executed this input epoch. Its retained
first-syscall result belongs to the preceding inputs and is historical.

The [name/glyph BEFORE study](battle-status-pairs-name-glyph-BEFORE-study.json)
explains all 71 invalid glyph-pointer failures: unconstructed Game name fields
retain the parent fixture's ambient byte. Native `1C9D60` reads four 20-byte,
ten-character big-endian name slots at Game+6604 and byte counts at +6654;
976 worlds have counts above ten. The actual first `A5A5` character maps to

match that native decoding exactly. This is not evidence for choosing a larger
font, clamping characters or manufacturing cached pointers. The next repair
must construct valid bounded names, retaining nonempty text paths, and restore
the font cache's genuine grid/list/capacity ownership.

The independent [font-state study](battle-status-pairs-font-state-study.json)
accounts for the other 163 faults: character+1C references cached-glyph metadata,
whose missing +8 grid pointer turns the rectangle argument into address four;
the native rectangle read then faults at address ten. `1C7408` creates 12-byte
glyph metadata with a real 32-byte grid-record owner. Startup `1C68F0(0x100,0x200)`
creates 21 by 12 grid records, with ordinal zero reserved as the circular-list
sentinel: that profile supports at most 251 resident glyphs. The rejected input
incorrectly marked all 1,413 font entries resident without grid or atlas ownership.
Completing this repair requires a bounded resident set and native allocation,
eviction and miss behavior; simply filling all missing pointers would retain an
invalid input state.

The [font/name/heap input repair](battle-status-pairs-font-BEFORE-preflight.json)

context, rather than a claim about exact default startup font selection.

The native heap uses fixed linear-list sentinels at `577F18/577F28`, ten real

VRAM records describe the palette, atlas and remaining free range. Their

131,072 bytes, and the `180` palette extent means 1,536 bytes. Allocation
ownership does not establish physical GS upload completion.

Independent native-graph review passed before emission. The complete 1,024-row

The first repaired run completes 768 worlds (725 returns and 43 clean syscall

One original byte, `326061`, becomes declared mutable RAM initialized to its

The [EE/IOP sound-worker study](battle-status-pairs-sound-worker-protocol-study.json)
follows the shipped `USERIRX/SDFSDMAN.IRX`, rather than inferring completion from
the managed HD sound path. The EE initializes a `0x5A0` status mirror and sends
its actual IOP address in command 0. The IOP registers SIF command 0, announces
readiness through SREG 31, and acknowledges handled commands through SREG 30
with the transported token. Its command `0xA0` loads the real SMG bank, copies
the header's bank ID/flags into seven eight-byte records at mirror+`0x190`,
then DMAs the mirror back to its stored EE address. The source-only
[wire layouts](../src/sound_worker_protocol.h) preserve packet, bank-record,
mirror and SREG-message fields. These are checked native protocol facts;
scheduler execution, file RPC and actual asynchronous arrival remain open.

The separate [actual original/C comparison](wakeup-kernel-C-validation.json)

This execution found a [C link-address defect](wakeup-kernel-link-semantics.json):
the saved kernel return address had four incorrectly sign-extended bytes.
The retained EE instruction manual, pages 66–68, specifies zero-extension of
JAL/JALR links and low32 register jump targets; pinned PCSX2
[link implementation](https://github.com/PCSX2/pcsx2/blob/b4d6e2aa7b9069162fa0e7cbc45ae95fc2566f39/pcsx2/Interpreter.cpp)
and [link macro](https://github.com/PCSX2/pcsx2/blob/b4d6e2aa7b9069162fa0e7cbc45ae95fc2566f39/pcsx2/R5900.h)
independently corroborate the rule. The corrected source retains upper GPR
lanes and selects the original low32 target before link/delay effects. This
architectural finding transfers to other SMT EE kernel and high-address callers;
it does not depend on Unity naming or a Nocturne-specific error reply.

## Battle outcome recovery publication

The [battle outcome publication](battle-outcome-publication.json) adds **23 complete functions**, bringing recovery to **2,746/9,062 functions (30.302%)**, **475,860 recognized bytes** and **552 source units**. These cover negotiation results, waiting, escape and return, with their motion, camera and press-packet prerequisites. All 3,840 original/probe/main cases pass over 3,559,264 original instruction steps, including 240 connected sequences and 4,176 completed calls. Each C replay compares 1,514,496,000 ordinary RAM bytes. All 23 native interfaces were saved, reopened and exported. The 12,100 affected retained cases and the 413-function/462,684-case broad suite pass on this module; all 550 earlier sources and objects remain unchanged.

Six entries consume raw F12 inputs, and one returns raw F0 separately from V0. Real process searches, packet allocation, recruitment, motion and RNG execute without supplied service results. The original process-name key and six diagnostic strings are included as read-only input resources. [Stored-results review](battle-outcome-results-review.json) and [coverage review](battle-outcome-coverage-study.json) distinguish complete 1,203-instruction source review from finite execution coverage: 948 of 964 non-delay positions were observed; 16 conditional positions remain unvisited. Two BREAK delay slots are source-proven annulled. The [callback route audit](battle-callback-route-audit.json) identifies eleven already-recovered callbacks that still need dispatcher wiring and entry-to-update lifecycle validation. Full executable recovery remains incomplete.

The [pinned ModernPressTurns source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NocturneMods/MatthiewNocturne/d641b7d07b7efb4af31210b46a65665a17dcb7c1/ModernPressTurns/ModernPressTurnsMod.cs) corroborates the WAIT/RETURN and press-packet workflow. Its altered HD costs, delays and managed fields are mod policy, not retail PS2 behavior. Native negotiation uses **Main+0x94**, while **Context+0x68** drives a separate dispatch. The packet layout is 64 bytes, and mixed F12/F0 ownership must survive transfer. Twenty-one HD counterparts are recorded as qualified role mappings; the unmatched failed-escape scan and the checker with corrupt HD pseudocode retain PS2 identifications. Five [shared SMT search fingerprints](shared-smt-signatures.tsv) require independent verification in every other title/build.
